Now I'm organizing the findings to write up the verdict, starting with the blocking issues. The first one concerns how the arrow order isn't being consumed by the arithmetic operation, and there's a mismatch in the fold order.\n\nThe code that handles the arrow ordering and contribution reduction has a critical flaw: it's using `localeCompare` without specifying an explicit locale, which means it resolves to whatever the host system's default ICU locale is. This makes the fold order environment-dependent, which violates the requirement for byte-identical reproduction in the replay executable. I can demonstrate this is genuinely significant—permuting the arrow order produces identical results, but renaming the opaque IDs shifts the output by one unit in the last place of the floating-point representation. The specification explicitly requires the arrow order to be recorded so that replay is exact, and this environment dependency breaks that guarantee.\n\nThe second blocking issue is that the transmission-reduction arithmetic lacks explicit rules for how the reduction should be applied. The code subtracts the reduction from the raw contribution, but the specification only defines the shape of what a reduction should be without ruling on whether it's subtractive or something else entirely.  On the test fixture, the two approaches yield different results (0.75 versus 0.78), and the handoff claims there are no invented numbers, which isn't accurate here. I need a ruling from the verification team or a citation to an existing rule before I can consider this path properly traced.\n\nThere are also some non-blocking issues: the `abstained` flag isn't handled consistently across different functions that check for unjudged nodes, the `isFolder` field has no producer anywhere in the codebase, and several fields are structurally always NULL despite being defined. The SCORING_LEVELS table also lacks a parity test between kernel and DDL definitions. There's a dead `continue` statement still present in the test suite that was flagged before but never removed, and it's still blocking a required string validation. The property tests for the FX-LV functions only compare trivial cases—a graph with no arrows against one with a single arrow—which doesn't really test the general behavior of adding arrows; stronger multi-arrow generators would be more convincing. The determinism test for FX-LV-03 also has a blind spot since it only runs in-process and can't catch cross-environment collation issues. I'm looking at the purity fence implementation... The typed refusals and error cases are well-defined (missing operator resolution, unimplemented replay shapes, empty product throws), and I'm verifying the three architectural axes all check out: the Both are proven against the real DB at `tests/integration/graph-database.test.ts:190-210`, not only against the stub in `tests/unit/graph-snapshot.test.ts`. P2 purity is real, not claimed: `packages/propagation/package.json` admits only `kernel` + `published-arithmetic`, both themselves fenced, and I confirmed zero clock/randomness/DB/model references in the core. Operator selection runs the full P8 chain (`packages/register/src/index.ts:56-71`) with the mandatory deployment row and closed-value validation, and the supplying level lands on every number — no source literal anywhere. AC-85 is intact — one arithmetic surface, pinned by the audit.\n\n**Why I am blocking anyway.** Two findings, one of them provable at the bit level.\n\nThe first is the ticket's own soul. P13 says every recomputation *consumes* the recorded arrow order — nobody re-derives. It doesn't. I permuted `arrowOrder` three ways over an identical graph and got a bit-identical result (`0.d751a037bb8c9` every time): the recorded order is inert for the arithmetic. What actually orders the `agg` fold is `localeCompare` over `JSON.stringify([target, polarity, \"singleton:\"+arrowId])` — an ICU-collated **opaque-id sort**, precisely what ADR-0005's ruled order exists to replace. I then held the graph and the recorded order fixed and renamed only the opaque ids: the served number moved one ulp (`…bb8c9` → `…bb8c8`). And `localeCompare` with no explicit locale resolves against the host default locale and ICU build, so the fold order — hence the last ulp — is environment-dependent across the separately-scheduled replay executable (ADR-0018 clause 2), which is the one thing manifest §4.2(a) says the recorded order exists to prevent. Reordering is genuinely ulp-significant; I found a witness (`0.9ea3306f66081` vs `0.9ea3306f6608`).\n\nThe second is smaller but needs V, not me. DR-071 rules the undercut's *shape* — \"a reduction of the targeted support edge's transmitted contribution\" — but no doc I can find rules whether that reduction is subtractive or multiplicative, nor the zero clamp. The code picked subtraction silently, and on the ticket's own fixture the two readings give 0.75 versus 0.78. The handoff's \"QUESTIONS FOR V: None\" is the part I can't sign.\n\nCLAUDE REVIEW: CHANGES REQUESTED\n- SOLID: greenlight — frozen strategy objects behind one interface, exhaustive selection, package responsibilities clean and the dependency closure mechanically fenced.\n- DDD: greenlight — kernel owns closed vocabulary, graph owns materialisation, register owns resolution, propagation owns pure arithmetic, ledger owns append-only receipts; no leakage across those seams.\n- TDD: greenlight — S03-progress.log RED is a real defect (folder-removal lift), not a staged failure; fixtures are behavioral, DB-relevant ones hit real Postgres, arithmetic ones are seeded fast-check.\n- Patterns (P-register): reject — P13 violated: the recorded arrow order is not consumed, and an ICU-collated opaque-id sort governs the fold instead (finding 1).\n- DR-115: greenlight — no fabricated strengths anywhere; unjudged and withheld nodes emit no number, and every unimplemented shape refuses loudly.\n- Findings:\n  1. **BLOCKING** `packages/propagation/src/index.ts:376,381,393,414-417,425` — recorded `arrowOrder` is not consumed by the aggregation (permuting it changes nothing); fold order is `localeCompare` collation of `singleton:${arrowId}`, so (a) the served number is a function of opaque ids — renaming ids alone moved it one ulp — and (b) collation depends on host locale/ICU build, breaking DR-034 replay exactness across the separate replay executable.
